# GSC Work Queue — TODO.md (v74 · 2026-11-09)

**Purpose.** The single work sink for the project: every future-work item any session surfaces — re-tests, rebuilds, backfills, corporate actions, queue seeds — routes **here** (routing per MODEL §7.1, v4.2.0). The register's own open items — pending user decisions and register-integrity holds, cap 7 — live in the head block `▶ Open items (register)` below (MODEL v4.5.0). This file is *not* authoritative state: `_REGISTER.md` wins on state, `MODEL.md` on rules, memo appendices on per-name detail. Nothing here is a verdict or a recommendation. Framework ideas go to `queue/IDEAS.md`, not here.

**Ticker discipline.** Tickers are suggested keys for convenience; resolved and verified at run time per §7.1a (prior-check on company name + ISIN, never the key alone).

## ▶ IDEAS candidates (attended triage)

*Copied verbatim from routine row files by INTAKE; attended triage moves each to `queue/IDEAS.md` or strikes it. INTAKE never clears this block.*

The 51 candidates of 2026-10-04/05 were triaged at the MODEL v4.5.0 build (2026-10-06): each is placed BUILT, CLOSED or PARKED (or struck) in `queue/IDEAS.md` §T (v11), rewritten to the final state after Jakub's method decisions J1–J15 (2026-10-06). The four lines below came with INTAKE 2026-10-07 on `main` (register v308) and were carried in by the sync merge of 2026-10-07; they await attended triage. The fourteen Gate-0 candidates of 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-14 (6383.T, RSW.L, QCOM, BA.L, 8002.T, INTU, ANDR.VI, IMI.L, DPLM.L, SKF-B.ST, ROCK-B.CO, BNZL.L, REL.L, EXPN.L) were closed by MODEL v4.6.0 (2026-10-15) and moved to `queue/IDEAS.md` §G0 (v14).

2026-10-30: the 181 candidates of 2026-10-05…10-29 were folded into `queue/IDEAS.md` v17 (map in the commit body); six method decisions plus G19 (two-run resume) taken (MODEL v4.8.0).

- 2026-10-30 · SGN.WA ANALYSE — Fiscal-year change: Sygnity's FY2023 ran 15 months (1 Oct 2022–31 Dec 2023). This run counted it as one fiscal year of the P1 window, as reported, without annualising, so it enters the 3-year averages, the per-share growth series and the trailing-3 pay and worth reads at 15 months. Should MODEL say how a transition period longer or shorter than 12 months enters P1, the averages and the YoY series?
- 2026-10-30 · JDG.L ANALYSE — Unit-level breadth: the filer states no customer exceeds 10% of group revenue but gives no per-segment statement; this run read each unit's breadth as silence (J13), flagging `customer conc.` on both units. Should a group-level statement clear a unit whose revenue is at least half the group's, or should MODEL say how a group statement maps to units?
- 2026-10-30 · JDG.L ANALYSE — Options without a printed grant-date fair value: R9 values them at shares × grant-date price, which for at-the-money options overstates the economic value several-fold. Should MODEL allow a printed IFRS 2 model's assumptions (here volatility, rate, life, dividend yield are printed) to price them, labelled, before falling back to shares × price?
- 2026-10-30 · RTO.L ANALYSE — Acquisition-stop proxy for serial bolt-ons: the IFRS 3 B64(q) acquiree operating profit is struck after the acquirer's own amortisation of the acquired intangibles and integration costs, so a year of small bolt-ons can read 60× (FY2024 here) even where the deals were priced on modest EBITDA multiples the filer does not print. Should MODEL say whether the proxy is read before acquired-intangible amortisation, or keep the post-amortisation reading as the conservative one?
- 2026-10-30 · RTO.L ANALYSE — Reporting-currency switch mid-window (GBP to USD from FY2025): this run translated FY2015–FY2022 at ECB annual averages (flows) and year-end closes (balances) and took the P20 deflator from each year's original reporting currency. Should MODEL fix the translation rates (filer's printed rates first?) and the deflator's home economy for such a window?
- 2026-10-30 · AT.L ANALYSE — A second price-endpoint outage in two days (DGE.L 2026-10-29): about 55 calls over 50 minutes, all HTTP 429, with Google Finance, Hargreaves Lansdown, uk.finance.yahoo.com and lse.co.uk also refusing the routine client. The run had every other input built, and its price-independent band legs set the trigger. Should an ANALYSE whose only missing input is px finish the memo at a dated close from a named fallback host, or should a run that meets an endpoint outage defer itself before claiming, rather than spend a queue slot on INCOMPLETE?
- 2026-10-30 · SUP.L ANALYSE — Brand purchases booked as intangible-asset purchases (asset acquisitions, not IFRS 3 business combinations): the capex definition counts every intangible purchase, and P20 removes only acquired revenue from growth, so FY2026's £12.9m SlimFast and 1001 trademark purchases sit in maintenance capex (tier 3). That reading sets MCap/FCF at 13.0 (FAIR) and creates the P17 flag that completes the SF flag pair; treating them as growth spend outside maintenance would give MCap/FCF 6.3 and SF PASS CAUTION (verdict AVOID either way, on Future). Should MODEL say whether an asset-deal brand purchase is capex or an acquisition read only on the acquisition stop?
- 2026-10-30 · SUP.L ANALYSE — Pre-listing per-share series: the run back-cast the group's 110,005,000 pre-admission shares to FY2017–FY2018 (Supreme Imports Limited's years before the 2018 merger-accounted reconstruction), labelled as an assumption. Should MODEL fix how per-share series start for a predecessor operating company's audited years (back-cast under merger accounting, or start the series at the listed entity's first share count)?
- 2026-10-30 · ATS.TO ANALYSE — Third price-endpoint outage in two days (DGE.L 2026-10-29, AT.L and ATS.TO 2026-10-30): 70 calls over 70 minutes, all HTTP 429. As with AT.L, every other input was built and the price-independent band legs already set the trigger (n.m.: debt-equivalents above 7 × OI). Should an ANALYSE whose only missing input is px take a fallback quote source or defer to a later slot instead of ending INCOMPLETE?
- 2026-10-30 · ATS.TO ANALYSE — Missing opening balance sheet: the FY2016 statements sat behind a Cloudflare-protected investor site and a 404 document-store path, so P1-window beginning-of-period averages would run FY2018–FY2026, while the complete five-year window already sets the governing level. Should README §5 (D3) say that an unretrieved input whose every possible value leaves the gate's grade unchanged is not INCOMPLETE?
- 2026-10-30 · ATS.TO ANALYSE — TTM maintenance capex: the interim prints no split of capitalised-development and software amortisation, so the P20 floor of a TTM construction could not be set and TTM took all capex. Should P20 allow the latest fiscal year's floor on TTM capex when the interim lacks the split?
- 2026-10-30 · DSG.TO ANALYSE — P20 deflator source: the run could not retrieve US CPI for FY2017–FY2024 (BLS public API daily threshold reached from the shared sandbox address; FRED refused the connection), so three years with printed acquired revenue fell to tier 3 (all capex). The effect here is under US$1.3m a year, but a name with heavier capex would move. Should MODEL name a fallback CPI source, or let a run use the 3% floor where the CPI cannot be retrieved and say so?
- 2026-10-31 · BYD.TO ANALYSE — Gate 2 timing mismatch after a large closed deal: the EV inputs come from the latest interim balance sheet (2026-06-30, carrying Joe Hudson's ~$1.0bn of new leases and debt), while OI and FCF stay on the latest fiscal year (FY2025, without the target's earnings). The rule is harsh by design, but should MODEL say whether a closed, filer-disclosed pro forma (business acquisition report) may move OI with EV for cheapness, as V11 allows for the sell line?
- 2026-10-31 · TVK.TO ANALYSE — SEDAR+-only filers (Canadian issuers with no SEC 40-F and no issuer-hosted statements) cannot be analysed by a routine: SEDAR+ blocks the sandbox with a bot-manager CAPTCHA (also seen at ATS.TO 2026-10-30). Should the queue header list "SEDAR+-only, no issuer-hosted statements" as a data-access exclusion, as `.KS` is, so the remaining `.TO` lines (SJ.TO, CMG.TO, ATD.TO) are checked for an issuer document store before they are queued?
- 2026-10-31 · SJ.TO ANALYSE — "stock still above worth" (§2·1C·3 ADVERSE limb) has no stated basis: this run read the current price on the trailing-3-FY point-in-time reads (MCap/FCF 16.8 above 16, EV/OI 10.5 below 14) and let the less flattering govern, while the latest-FY read (MCap/FCF 8.2) is far below worth. Should MODEL name the read the live-stock limb uses?
- 2026-10-31 · CMG.TO ANALYSE — The third price-endpoint outage in three days (DGE.L 2026-10-29, AT.L 2026-10-30): about 70 calls over 66 minutes, all HTTP 429, beginning at the run's first call. The memo was complete except px; the gates already decided the verdict (Past, Future and SF each fail), so the missing price could not have moved it. Should a run whose verdict is settled without Gate 2 finish at a dated close from a named fallback host, or defer itself before claiming when the endpoint is down?
- 2026-10-31 · ATD.TO ANALYSE — the acquisition stop read the FY2024 TotalEnergies + MAPCO deals on the filer's B64(q) and pro-forma net-earnings contribution (68.6 after tax and financing → ≈ 57× EV/OI, ADVERSE), while the MD&A prints a pre-acquisition EBITDA (≈ 8.9× EV/EBITDA, synergies included, unaudited). For a large carve-out bought mid-winter, first-months contributions carry integration costs and seasonality: should MODEL let a printed pre-deal EBITDA less a disclosed D&A bound the proxy, or require a second-year read before ADVERSE?
- 2026-10-31 · ATD.TO ANALYSE — the P15 silence rule flagged `customer conc.` on a mass retailer because dealer, bulk and fleet fuel sales are unquantified (J13 read organisations as ≥ 10% of revenue). Should the silence rule apply where the filer's organisational sales are not shown to reach 10% of revenue?
- 2026-10-31 · KPG.AX ANALYSE — Partner-NCI roll-ups (KPG.AX, ANALYSE 2026-10-31): minorities took 80% of FY2026 profit, so MCap/FCF on consolidated FCF reads 5.8 against about 29 on the shareholders' share; EV/OI carries NCI at book and binds here, but a name where it does not could pass MCap/FCF on the partners' cash. Question: should Gate 2 read MCap/FCF on FCF attributable to owners where NCI takes a material share of profit?
- 2026-10-31 · KRZ.IR ANALYSE — P29 weight base when the filer's latest segmentation has fewer than three fiscal years (KRZ.IR: regional segments from FY2025, FY2024 re-presented): the revenue fallback is equally undisclosed for the third year; this run weighted on the two years that exist, logged. Should P29 name that case?
- 2026-10-31 · KRX.IR ANALYSE — kingspan.com and kingspangroup.com sit behind an Anubis proof-of-work bot check, so a routine reads annual reports from archive mirrors and cannot reach the full half-yearly report; EV was built on the audited year-end balance sheet with the interim net-debt line as a cross-check. Should MODEL §4 state when an unretrievable interim balance sheet is a D3 gap versus a logged fallback to the latest audited balance sheet in ANALYSE?
- 2026-10-31 · OIZ.IR ANALYSE — Origin discloses trade payables "known to have been sold onward" by its suppliers to factors (€63.9m at FY2026, Note 20, presented as supplier finance arrangements); the run read them as supplier-finance liabilities (debt-equivalent, out of NIBCL) under J13. Should MODEL §2 Gate 2 state whether supplier-initiated factoring of the company's payables, with unchanged payment terms, is a supplier-finance liability?
- 2026-10-31 · COR.LS ANALYSE — Corticeira Amorim's only customer statement ("~25 thousand customers … none representing more than 2% of total sales") sits in the credit-risk note; the run read it as a customer statement because it states each customer's share of sales, not of receivables. Should MODEL §2·1B say whether a sales-share statement inside a credit-risk note counts?
- 2026-10-31 · COR.LS ANALYSE — the P17 trailing proportionality read met a negative three-year FCF leg (FY2022–FY2024) with a positive fiscal-year leg; the run read any pay as above a negative line (a flag), since the loss-year limb is written for the fiscal-year leg only. Should P17 state how a trailing leg ≤ 0 is read?
- 2026-10-31 · PERSISTENT.NS ANALYSE — P20 tier 2 needs a home-CPI deflator from a named official source for each reporting currency; a standing table of the fiscal-year CPI rows (INR first: MOSPI all-India CPI) would let routine runs build the constructed tier without an extra lookup (PERSISTENT.NS ANALYSE 2026-10-31 fell back to `= all capex`).
- 2026-10-31 · PERSISTENT.NS ANALYSE — P17 on Indian filers: the Corporate Governance report's Table 9 prints pay on a "paid" basis that includes the perquisite value of options exercised (realised pay); the grant-date basis sits in the ESOP note. A note in §2·1C·1 on reading both for Indian filers would make the hairiest number repeatable (PERSISTENT.NS 2026-10-31).
- 2026-10-31 · PAGEIND.NS ANALYSE — P20 tier 2 for Indian filers: the routine runs (PERSISTENT.NS, PAGEIND.NS) fall back to all capex for want of a named home-CPI series by fiscal year; a standing MOSPI CPI table in the repo would let the constructed tier run.
- 2026-10-31 · PIDILITIND.NS ANALYSE — P19 materiality floor at the edge: PIDILITIND.NS's FY2018 tender bought 0.98% of the year's average diluted shares (0.975% of pre-buyback capital). The run read "about 1%" as met at one-decimal rounding (1.0%, J13), which turns a lone pay flag into a flag pair and the verdict from WATCH to AVOID. A stated rounding rule for the floor (e.g. ≥ 0.95%, or strictly ≥ 1.0% unrounded) would make the call repeatable.
- 2026-10-31 · VINATIORGA.NS ANALYSE — Acquisition stop on a pooled common-control amalgamation (VINATIORGA.NS, VAPL 2024): the target's pre-deal accounts are a private company's and not published by the acquirer; the run read "no positive pre-deal OI shown" as CAUTION, while VAPL's reserves rose INR 5.4m in FY2022 — if any positive OI existed, EV/OI would be far above 25 (ADVERSE). A rule for which reading governs when the target's OI is unpublished but the acquirer's notes show a pre-operational plant would settle it.
- 2026-10-31 · VINATIORGA.NS ANALYSE — P20 tier 2 for Indian filers (again, VINATIORGA.NS): a standing MOSPI CPI table by fiscal year in the repo would let the constructed tier run; here it may move Past between WEAK and ADEQUATE.
- 2026-10-31 · DIVISLAB.NS ANALYSE — P20 deflator for Indian filers (DIVISLAB.NS): this run used the World Bank calendar-year India CPI of the calendar year ending inside the fiscal year (FY_t ← CY_{t−1}) as the "fiscal year's consumer-price inflation"; a standing fiscal-year MOSPI CPI table in the repo, or an explicit rule for the calendar-year mapping, would make the constructed tier repeatable across Indian names (VINATIORGA.NS ran without one).
- 2026-10-31 · DIVISLAB.NS ANALYSE — A refundable customer advance funding dedicated capacity (DIVISLAB.NS, INR 4,560m at FY2026, a non-current financial liability at amortised cost): the run read it as a Gate 2 debt-equivalent (repayable money, J13); the debt-equivalent list does not name customer-funded refundable advances, and the deferred-revenue part (INR 4,020m) stayed out of NIBCL on burden of proof. A named rule for both would settle the reading.
- 2026-10-31 · AIAENG.NS ANALYSE — Related-party sales to a promoter-family trading entity (AIAENG.NS: AB Tradelink, INR 2,887m of sales in FY2026, receivable INR 1,583m, only amounts and an arm's-length statement disclosed): this run read it as one RPT flag (material, thin detail). §2·1C·2's tests are written for payments to insiders; a named rule for sales to a controller's entity (what disclosure makes it checkable, and whether a stretched receivable from it is its own finding) would make the reading repeatable.
- 2026-10-31 · CUMMINSIND.NS ANALYSE — Payments to a controlling parent labelled "support services" or "royalty" with no printed scope (CUMMINSIND.NS: INR 2,130.1m support services and INR 779.4m royalty to Cummins Inc. in FY2026): the run read the support-services fee as a glaring RPT (support is a function management is already paid to perform; the harsher of strike and thin-detail flag, J13) and the royalty as a thin-detail flag of the same behaviour. A named rule for parent technology royalties and group service charges in listed subsidiaries (strike, flag, or a share-of-expense test) would make the reading repeatable across MNC subsidiaries in the queue (ABBOTINDIA.NS, CRISIL.NS carry the same note).
- 2026-10-31 · CUMMINSIND.NS ANALYSE — A reportable segment that is an equity-accounted joint venture reported at 100% of its own figures (CUMMINSIND.NS Lubes, Valvoline Cummins): the run treated it as a material unit on segment sales (20.1%) and weighted it on its segment PBT as the filer reports it; P22 and P29 do not say whether such a segment is a unit, a G6 class only, or weighted at the parent's economic share.
- 2026-10-31 · ELGIEQUIP.NS ANALYSE — J6 limb (a) on the cash-inclusive ROCE series of a net-cash compounder (ELGIEQUIP.NS: ROCE_wc 22.3% → 19.2% → 18.5% → 18.0% over FY2023–FY2026 while ROCE_xc held at 26%–27%; cash and deposits rose from INR 2.8bn to INR 8.7bn): the decline fires on cash accumulation alone and, with the ten-year level at 14.1%, makes Past WEAK. Whether limb (a) should read a decline that the ex-cash series does not show is a calibration question for Jakub.
- 2026-11-01 · CRISIL.NS ANALYSE — Common-control customer aggregation against a filer's entity-wide statement (CRISIL.NS): the group note says no customer reaches 10% of revenue, while services to the controller's group entities (S&P Global) sum to 13.0% of FY2025 revenue (derived from the related-party note). §2·1B counts entities under common control as one customer, and the v4.8.0 group-statement rule lets a group statement cover its units. A one-line rule on whether a related-party table that sums above 10% under common control counts as "the filer names a concentrated customer" would make the reading repeatable.
- 2026-11-01 · ABBOTINDIA.NS ANALYSE — Reciprocal group service charges in a listed subsidiary (ABBOTINDIA.NS: INR 73.60 crore paid to Abbott Healthcare Private Ltd for "shared and support services" in FY2026, while the company earned INR 64.80 crore of shared-services income from the same fellow subsidiary; AR26 note 39): should reciprocal charges with the same group entity net before the P27 glaring-RPT test? CUMMINSIND.NS read a parent's support-services fee gross as a strike.
- 2026-11-01 · ABBOTINDIA.NS ANALYSE — Marketing rights for products held by the parent's affiliates and granted at no price (ABBOTINDIA.NS AR26 note 39): P15's single-licence rule needs the licence's share of revenue, which the filer does not print. A rule for undisclosed parent-held brand rights would settle whether they are read in breadth or in the supplier-power force.
- 2026-11-02 · TCS.NS ANALYSE — §2·1C·2 controller as counterparty: a controller that tenders its pro-rata entitlement into an open tender-offer buyback (Indian tender buybacks; TCS.NS FY2018–FY2024, Tata Sons the largest seller each time) is read here as selling shares to the company, so a tender clearly above worth is ADVERSE. A line in §2·1C·2 on whether a pro-rata tender open to all holders falls under the limb would make the reading repeatable (TCS.NS ANALYSE 2026-11-02).
- 2026-11-02 · TCS.NS ANALYSE — §2·1C·2 glaring RPT: Tata group companies pay Tata Sons a "brand equity contribution" for the Tata name (TCS.NS FY2026 INR 368 crore, 0.78% of FCF). The rule names "licensing the controller's name" as a glaring RPT, so every material Tata group payer strikes; a register-wide note would keep later Tata group memos consistent (TCS.NS ANALYSE 2026-11-02).
- 2026-11-04 · RXL.PA REVIEW — Point-in-time worth reads (v4.8.0) average the trailing three fiscal years' J1 OI, so a write-down year inside them (RXL.PA: FY2020 OI −9.5 after EUR 486.0m of goodwill impairment) lifts the read for every tranche bought in the next three years; at RXL.PA it puts the FY2023 second-half tranche at 14.1 no-cash, a hair over the worth line, where the in-doubt rule decides the flag. Worth an attended look at whether the P28 down-year logic should reach the worth read (REVIEW 2026-11-04).
- 2026-11-04 · BG.VI ANALYSE — FI lens: the FI gains rule names "gains on securities and disposals" but a bank's P&L line "gains and losses on financial assets and liabilities" mixes derecognition gains with hedge and FV results (banks keep trading income); BG.VI 2026-11-04 removed the whole line pre-tax as the harsher reading — a fixed rule for which sub-lines leave NI would make FI memos repeatable
- 2026-11-04 · AVARDA.ST ANALYSE — FI lens funding test: a deposit platform (Raisin) is one counterparty under P15, but filers rarely print deposits by channel; AVARDA.ST 2026-11-04 inferred the platform share from deposit-only countries plus the platform's own product pages (harsher reading, J13) — a fixed evidence standard for platform-sourced deposits would make the funding test repeatable across banks
- 2026-11-05 · PYPL REVIEW — §2·1A Segregated customer funds — "cash and cash equivalents" inside customer accounts can include partner deposits and short AFS (PYPL 2026-11-05 netted the line as reported, less the footnoted AFS): should cash equivalents count as held safely only where the filer shows them as bank deposits or money-market funds?

- 2026-11-05 · KGX.DE ANALYSE — Funding pairing in the register: KGX.DE carries pairing A (lease and rental funding in EV, OI before its funding cost) in its core fields as the governing, higher EV/OI; a fixed rule for which pairing the `core_` fields carry would keep captive-finance names comparable
- 2026-11-05 · JUN3.DE ANALYSE — Aggregate acquisition-stop read with a loss-making start-up: annualising Magazino's €−4.5m (130 days) inside JUN3.DE's FY2023 aggregate turns a Storage Solutions read of 12.1× into 19.8× (37.9× without amortisation add-backs); a note on whether a loss-making target with consideration below 1% of EV should enter the year's aggregate at its annualised loss would make the stop's intent explicit
- 2026-11-05 · JUN3.DE ANALYSE — Funding pairing in the core fields: JUN3.DE carries pairing B (funding out of EV, TTM OI after funding cost, all inputs published) while KGX.DE carries pairing A; the governing band is pairing A in both — a fixed rule for which pairing the `core_` fields carry would keep captive-finance names comparable

- 2026-11-04 · NOVO-B.CO REVIEW — The repurchase materiality floor is "about 1%" of the year's average diluted shares; NOVO-B.CO's live 2026 tranche stands at 0.94% with the programme still running, read here as at the floor. - 2026-11-06 · LGCY ANALYSE — Short records that open with loss years: in a history-limited record (here FY2019–FY2026, a 2024 IPO), two opening operating-loss years make ΔOI/sh n.m. on both reads (the first-three-year average is ≤ 0), so Past fails P3 on `growth` while ROCE (15.5%) and FCFROCE (5.6%) clear the P3(b) levels, and the finding persists until the loss years leave a window that is still filling up (FY2029 results). SDI.L's FY2025 aggregate reads 25.7× gross and 19.6× net, straddling the clearly-above-worth line; the memo let the less flattering govern (ADVERSE). The memo read the printed level as at the floor and gave `ROCE decline` as the Past reason (the decline decides WEAK either way). Should the bars be tested on the unrounded average, and which reason token then leads?

## ▶ Group dependencies

*INTAKE maintains this block (MODEL §2 Group dependencies; §8.7 item 6; `routines/INTAKE.md` step 3). **v4.6.0 (G4): a dependency never stops its parent.** A group run (a parent with a material controlled listed subsidiary or listed holding) completes with a part whose memo is missing or stale read as ungradeable (WEAK with its weight in the Future, nothing added to the look-through Past, no credit) and lists it; INTAKE adds `` `HKEY` — dependency of PARENT (run {D}) ``, removes it when HKEY's ANALYSE or REVIEW memo lands, and then adds `` `PARENT` — parent re-run owed (HKEY memo {date}) `` for REVIEW priority 1c. ANALYSE takes an unmarked key with no memo first; REVIEW takes a stale one (memo older than 15 months, written below MODEL v4.5.0, or a DNAF memo written below v4.6.0) at priority 1c. A marked key stays ungradeable for its parents. `INCOMPLETE (dependency: KEY)` and verdict invariance are retired; before v4.6.0 a line was added when a group run ended `INCOMPLETE (dependency: KEY)`.*

## ▶ Method re-tests v4.8.0

*Method rules that can move a grade, an SF result, a trigger or a verdict (MODEL v4.8.0: Jakub's method decisions of 2026-10-29/30; `queue/IDEAS.md` §T2), effective at "commit model" and applied at a name's next ANALYSE or REVIEW, never retroactively. REVIEW reads this block at priority 1b, before `▶ Method re-tests v4.6.0` and `▶ Method re-tests v4.5.0`: held names first, from all three blocks, then the partial resumes, oldest first, then the top line first, block by block (`routines/REVIEW.md` step 2). INTAKE removes a key's line when that key's ANALYSE or REVIEW memo written under MODEL v4.8.0 or later lands, which also removes the key's v4.6.0 and v4.5.0 lines, and marks it when the run ends INCOMPLETE (MODEL §8.7 item 6). A memo below v4.8.0 is re-tested on the v4.8.0 rules (MODEL §8.2 step 3), and a key that also has a v4.6.0 or v4.5.0 line takes those re-tests in the same run. The rules, by the tag each line uses: `unit statement` (§2·1B, P15) — a group statement or customer table that rules out any customer at 10% of consolidated revenue, or at a lower threshold the filer states, covers every material unit, unless the filer names a concentrated customer or unit or a unit discloses its own concentration; it clears the breadth flag only, and each unit's customer-power force is still called on its own evidence; `growth (ii)` (§2·1A; P2, P3, P24) — where the geometric read (i) is n.m., the endpoint read (ii) governs every test on the metric, and a metric n.m. on both reads fails; `customer funds` (§2·1A, Gate 2) — segregated customer funds held safely (cash, deposits, money-market funds or government securities maturing within 12 months, as reported) and their printed obligation leave CE, once, and L/E; the funds are never cash, the obligation is never a debt-equivalent, float income booked in revenue stays in OI, and nothing is netted where the filer does not show the split; `deal proxy` (§2·1C·3, P19) — an acquiree profit printed after tax or after the deal's acquired-intangible amortisation is grossed up to an OI basis, adding back only a charge it actually bore and only where its rate or amount is printed (tax at the acquirer's printed effective rate for the profit's fiscal year; the deal's printed amortisation), else the printed figure is used, labelled the conservative reading; `buyback timing` (§2·1C·3, P19) — each tranche-year is read at the point of purchase, on the trailing-3-FY averages whose annual reports were published before it and the latest balance sheet published before it, while the year read, a later TTM and later restatements are printed beside, never governing; `per-year floor` (§2·1C·3, P19) — the ≈ 1% materiality floor is tested per fiscal year, never summed across years, a year's sterilising purchases counting with its ordinary ones. P17's compensation actually paid stays strike-capable (reaffirmed) and has no line here. Each line reads `KEY` — memo vN (date, MODEL vX) {tags}: what the memo shows, with its section → REVIEW on v4.8.0 rules. Turned: the memo's grade, SF result, trigger or verdict rested on the reading a rule changes. Possible: the memo reads a changed rule, but whether a grade moves is open. The figures are memo text (a derived figure is labelled): pointers for the REVIEW, never findings. A verdict change goes to Jakub for the merge. Landing scan of 2026-10-30 over every current memo of the release base (43577b9), with TROW read on memo v9 (PR #303), and over the six ANALYSE memos merged after it (PRs #304–#315, MODEL v4.7.0), deduped against the v4.8.0 instances in `queue/IDEAS.md` v17: every listed key has one current memo and none is RETIRED. Three of the six qualify, REY.MI (turned: a verdict may move), RTO.L (turned: Future) and JDG.L (possible), but their ANALYSE rows were still pending at this commit, so their lines wait for the attended queue edit after INTAKE 2026-10-31 (`▶ Routed by the attended v4.8.0 queue commit, register unchanged (2026-10-30)`). Not listed, because no v4.8.0 rule can move their grade, SF result or trigger: for growth (ii), EQNR (L/E binds), ZBRA (a J6 decline with the levels below P2), BEIJ-B.ST (ROCE below the 10% bar) and SGN.WA (ΔOI/sh n.m. on both reads, so it fails; ten-year ROCE 8.5%), while OXIG.L and CNM, whose read (ii) now governs, stay WEAK on a J6 decline with the levels below P2 (their lines below are for other rules); for the unit statement, 5908.KL (no revenue-concentration statement at any level: its credit-risk note speaks of receivables); for customer funds, MA, V and NU (immaterial to the grade); for the SF rules, QCOM and BRK.B (the SF result is unchanged), BA.L (its strike, Ball Aerospace, is read on pre-deal operating earnings, and its clearly-above H1 2026 tranche is below the floor on its own), GEBN.SW (the buybacks CAUTION and the director-pay flag pair on any reading) and SUP.L (Clearly Drinks' after-tax proxy is grossed up, but Typhoo's pre-deal loss and SlimFast's undisclosed contribution keep the `acquisitions` flag, which pairs with pay on any reading); held, HFG.DE (its buybacks CAUTION also rests on the FY2022 earlier-window tranche under the same CEO, clearly above worth on point-in-time reads too, MCap/FCF ≈ 30× and EV/OI ≈ 39–40× on its B1 FY2019–21 averages and the year-end EV, derived, so the flag pair with pay stands; Past WEAK on the level, the decline and leverage), NOVO-B.CO (its strike rests on the 2023 tranche, clearly above worth on point-in-time reads too, ≈ 51× FY2020–22 maintenance FCF and ≈ 1.1% of shares, both derived from its B1; without it the director-pay and acquisitions flags pair) and CROX (step 1 is the HEYDUDE write-down).*

## ▶ Method re-tests v4.6.0

*Gate-0, lens and group rules that reopen DNAF names and void DNAF expectations (MODEL v4.6.0: Jakub's decisions G1–G6 of 2026-10-15; `queue/IDEAS.md` §G0), effective at "commit model". REVIEW reads this block at priority 1b before `▶ Method re-tests v4.5.0` — held names first, from both blocks, then the top line first; INTAKE removes a key's line when that key's ANALYSE or REVIEW memo written under MODEL v4.6.0 or later lands, and marks it when the run ends INCOMPLETE (MODEL §8.7 item 6). A memo below v4.6.0 re-tests Gate 0, the lens, the group rules and the G6 pairing on v4.6.0, and a prior DNAF on a retired ground runs the full analysis (MODEL §8.2 step 3); a key that also has a `▶ Method re-tests v4.5.0` line takes that re-test in the same run. The v4.6.0 reading: the understanding statement is the best sentence the filings allow, and Gate 0 fails only for incomprehension or obfuscation (G1, G2); the irreducible-unit test with its J13 named-without-shares limb, the material-"Other" fail, the holdings-cycle fail and the R4 too-hard pile retire, the three too-hard cases reading on STD with its conservative rules (G2); the material unit (P22) is the grain, each with its own sentence and Future block, and a unit that mixes businesses, or an "Other", is described naming its parts and graded as one unit (G3); conglomerates, trading houses and holding companies are analysed at the parent — consolidated Past, each material part's Future, SF with the allocation across the parts (`reinvestment`) — and a part never fails the group nor stops the parent (G4). In Gate 1A, every G6 class above the P31 line (equity-accounted associates and JVs with their long-term interests, H excepted; S3 stakes, securities and non-operating loans; an insurance activity's whole investment portfolio) is read exclude-both and include-both, the harsher governing (G6); the G6 sub-heading lists the names the landing scan found above the line. Each line reads `KEY` — the memo and its retired v4.5.x ground → run the full analysis (G1–G4) → where the worked figures sit. A projection, where one exists, is the 2026-10-14 Gate-0 review's reading if Gate 0 opened, on the v4.5.3 rules below it: a pointer, never a finding; Appendix G of each memo records the move. A verdict change goes to Jakub for the merge.*

None: no held name rests on a retired v4.5.x ground.

## ▶ Method re-tests v4.5.0

*Codified rules that can move a verdict (MODEL v4.5.0: Jakub's decisions J1–J15 of 2026-10-06 as revised by his round-2 decisions R1–R14 and V1–V18 of 2026-10-07; `queue/IDEAS.md` §T), effective at "commit model". REVIEW reads this block as priority 1b, top line first; INTAKE removes a key's line when that key's REVIEW memo of v4.5.0 or later lands, and marks it when the run ends INCOMPLETE (MODEL §8.7 item 6). Order: held names; upward moves (expected, then possible); WATCH → AVOID by confidence (≥ 80%, then 60–75%); outcome open (below 60%); verdict-word changes to DNAF last. **Re-scanned line by line under round 2 on 2026-10-07**, from memo evidence (Appendices B, D and E) and the round-2 area specs: each line reads `KEY` — the rules that bite: what the memo shows → expected (confidence). The figures are memo text ("verify" where the memo lacks one; "ext." marks outside knowledge, never used as a number): pointers for the REVIEW, never findings. The round-2 reading: two lenses, and the too-hard pile (Gate 0 FAIL, DNAF) for a bank or insurer that does not fit FI, a group with a material non-captive insurer or deposit-funded bank, and anything neither lens reads (R4); captive finance arms consolidated on STD (R2); groups on the consolidated Past and the OI-weighted Future (P29; R3, R5); material listed holdings at their own intrinsic value (V14); maintenance-capex FCF in every FCF test, the all-capex reading printed beside it (P20, R6); the P28 one-off down year for price, the trigger, the ADD band and the sell line, with mitigating facts in the sell evaluation (R1, V11); customer and supplier concentration flagged and judged against the moat, a concentration without a strong moat being a STRONG force under P16 (R7); pay on the hairiest number, P17's lines and the director cap as flags and a strike only when egregious, more than twice the line (R9, V3); pledges and sales never flags, the alignment call (P23) and votes versus economics inputs that act only at strike-logic step 3, where a single flag fails (`alignment`) with NOT ALIGNED management and separated votes (R8, V4); sterilisation below about 1% of shares logged, ADVERSE only when egregious and dilutive (V13); acquisitions against worth and the glaring-RPT strike confirmed (V1, V17); the trigger at the no-cash bound (R10). Worth yardsticks (P19, STD): at or below about 14× EV/OI or 16× MCap/FCF is at worth; about 25× EV/OI or 28.6× MCap/FCF is clearly above it; where a tranche's reads straddle a yardstick the less flattering governs when in doubt, the FCF read being the maintenance-capex one. Flags count per distinct behaviour (repurchases, deals, executive pay, director fees). A verdict change goes to Jakub for the merge. Label-only, section-only and band-only moves are not listed here (`▶ Routed by the MODEL v4.5.0 build` below). **Re-read under MODEL v4.6.0 (2026-10-15):** the too-hard pile and the one-level decomposition are retired (G2, G3) and a missing dependency never stops a parent (G4), so no line below expects DNAF or `INCOMPLETE (dependency)` any more; the lines that did (BRK.B, ULVR.L, CSU.TO and the R4 and decomposition names) are rewritten in place, and the DNAF ones also have a line in `▶ Method re-tests v4.6.0`, which REVIEW reads first.*

6. **Retrieval note — EDGAR `Financial_Report.xlsx` is the cheap primary route for IFRS 20-F filers.** The XBRL workbook of each 6-K/20-F (`/Archives/edgar/data/{cik}/{accession}/Financial_Report.xlsx`) returns the full statements first and every note after, in the reporting currency, and the fetch tool renders it as HTML; a decade of Novo (FY2015–FY2025, 11 balance sheets) was rebuilt from five of them plus two results releases. URLs must first be surfaced by a search (the tool refuses unseen URLs) — searching the indexed title form `"Quarterly Earnings Tables - quarter ended Dec YYYY"` / `"Annual Financial Statement Tables - full year YYYY"` works. The company-announcement HTML (`caqNYYYY.htm`) truncates before the appendices for the 40-page year-end releases even at a 90k limit; the shorter interim releases (≤37 pp) get through. Append to the retrieval doctrine (block D).

## ▶ Routed from the AMZN v3 fresh ANALYSE, register v294 (2026-09-13)

1. **`AMZN` — FY2025 realized compensation not retrieved (P17 logged INDETERMINATE).** The 2026 proxy discloses a realized-compensation figure beside the Summary Compensation Table total; only the SCT total was sourced (**Jassy $2.1m**; no equity granted to any NEO in 2025, none to the CEO since 2021). Realized comp rises with the share price and is the **only** route by which the ≈US$40m indexed absolute-scrutiny trigger could fire. Proportionality is not at issue — it passes by roughly three orders of magnitude on both reads. **Named re-test: the 2027 DEF 14A (~Apr 2027).** Under §2·1C an INDETERMINATE finding can never complete a flag pair, so this cannot fail SF on its own.
2. **`AMZN` — FY2025 director compensation totals not retrieved.** Amazon pays non-employee directors in restricted stock with no cash retainer; Bezos and Jassy take none. Historical grant levels sit well inside the US 2026 stops (chair/lead ≈$1.02m, ordinary ≈$672k), so a hard strike is implausible, but the figure is owed and the memo's Appendix E flag stays open until it is sourced. **Re-test: 2027 DEF 14A.**
3. **`AMZN` — P19 CAUTION re-test; SF now one flag from failing.** SF PASSes on a single flag (capital allocation CAUTION); one further flag pairs and fails the gate. Discriminating evidence for the next touch, in order: (a) whether any repurchase authorisation is **created or executed** — there has been none since the single $6.0bn FY2022 tranche, and conjunct (iii) of ADVERSE turns on exactly this; (b) whether FY2026 capex lands near the ~$200bn guide; (c) whether the debt-funded share keeps rising — long-term debt face went **$68,836m → $132,995m in H1 2026** on $66,998m of gross issuance across USD/EUR/CHF/CAD; (d) whether AWS growth and the ~36.8% trailing segment margin hold through the ramp. **Triggers: Q3 2026 results (30 Oct 2026), FY2026 10-K (~Feb 2027), 2027 proxy.**
4. **`AMZN` — current lease liabilities and short-term debt are not disclosed on the balance-sheet face.** Amazon presents only three current-liability lines (payables, accrued expenses and other, unearned revenue). The current portion of long-term debt ($2,748m at FY2025 year-end, from the Q1 2026 debt note), short-term debt and current lease liabilities all sit inside "accrued expenses and other". Two consequences, both disclosed rather than corrected because correcting requires estimating an undisclosed input: **(i)** NIBCL is overstated and capital employed understated by an estimated $16–19bn, biasing ROCE **up** by ~0.4pp on a $600bn base — against conservatism, and immaterial to the gate (worst-of-four 11.1% vs a 10% floor); **(ii)** the headline EV uses only the primary-sourced debt stack of $226,562m and omits ~$25,073m, which **flatters** the band by roughly $2–3 — the conservative stack is printed alongside in Appendix F. **Closure: the FY2026 10-K lease note (current operating and finance lease liabilities) and debt note.**
5. **`AMZN` — `stockanalysis.com` carries a restated FY2022 operating income.** It prints **13,348** against the filed **12,248**. The series was therefore **rejected** for the P1 window under §4's exact-tie-out rule and the decade was rebuilt from `marketbeat.com`, which tied out *exactly* against the FY2025 Q4 8-K on both covered years (revenue, OI, equity, and the current- and non-current-liability composites). Recorded so a later session does not silently re-adopt the restated series. The underlying SEC-retrieval constraint is already logged under the PYPL v5 block below (items 3–4) and is **not duplicated here** — but note it bit again: every primary this session required its own locator search, and the 8-K earnings-release exhibits were again the cheap route to full statements.
6. **`AMZN` — other acquired intangibles not retrieved.** Tangible book value is computed as equity less goodwill only, which slightly **overstates** TBV. Immaterial against $411bn of equity and $23.3bn of goodwill; both qualifiers fail by a wide margin either way. Backfill from the goodwill-and-intangibles note at the next touch.

1. **`PYPL` — FY2024 balance-sheet extraction conflict, §4.** The carried B1 row prints total assets $81,611m / NIBCL $48,376m / total liabilities $61,194m; the FY2025 4Q 8-K comparative column prints **$78,725m / $45,490m / $58,308m** — a $2,886m gross-up on both sides, with equity, cash+STI and goodwill+intangibles identical. Never reaches the 10% floor, so Past stays ADEQUATE at any plausible magnitude. Closure needs the FY2020–FY2024 10-K balance sheets. FY2025 and the 30 Jun 2026 interim are already clean.

Route that survives: 8-K earnings-release exhibits, which carry two years of full statements each — five releases would rebuild the decade. Worth doing at the FY2026 10-K touch.
4. **Retrieval note — SEC 10-K fetches truncate.** `www.sec.gov/Archives/.../pypl-20241231.htm` returned Items 1 and 1A and stopped well before the financial statements even at a 30k-token limit. ### D. Method notes from the runs — retrieval routes that worked or failed (append to the retrieval doctrine)

- **FX:** `https://api.frankfurter.dev/v1/{YYYY-MM-DD}?from={BASE}&to={CCY}` — ECB euro reference rates; use the `.dev` host and `/v1/` path, percent-encode commas, **record the returned `date` field** (TARGET holidays return the prior publication). Label cells "ECB ref, {date}", never as a venue close. Fallback `open.er-api.com/v6/latest/{base}` is latest-only. `www.ecb.europa.eu` itself is refused from the cloud sandbox.
- **Google Finance price route:** the `Closed:` prefix is US-only; for every other venue the discriminator is **stamp time ≥ the venue's session end** (Taipei 05:30 UTC, Tokyo 06:00, Hong Kong ~08:10). **Always fetch with a unique cache-buster query parameter** — 9 of ~70 bare fetches returned months-old quotes with plausible magnitudes (ADBE 246.00 vs 257.26; NU 12.99 vs 15.33; TRI 86.04 vs 98.81). Tencent key is `0700:HKG` (`700:HKG` 404s). Provider snapshot stamps (MarketScreener `HH:MM:SS YYYY-MM-DD`) date the page, not the trade. A guessed numeric provider ID returned Per Aarsleff for a Carlsberg request — **record the instrument name with every price**.
- **Provider market caps use the ISSUED share count (incl. treasury); value per share on the NET count** — tested exactly on Royal Unibrew (issued 49,300,000; treasury 1,236,387 = 2.5%; provider cap reconciles to 0.02% on issued). One division per row — `price × shares = displayed market cap` — caught two real stale counts (`DNP.WA`, `BKNG`) that no identity check can catch (ISIN-stable splits are invisible to the §7.1a prior-check). Make it standing on every SWEEP (IDEAS C5). MarketScreener's Diageo market cap was 38% off on this test.
- **SEC:** the R-file route `…/{cik}/{accession-no-dashes}/R{n}.htm` renders one statement per file — fetch `FilingSummary.xml` first (lists every R-file with ShortName); **`R1.htm` is the cover page and the cheapest primary share count**. No R-file exists for Item 5; the 10-K XBRL instance truncates inside its context definitions. EDGAR full-text search is **ranked and truncated — a nil return is not evidence of absence**; `/cgi-bin/browse-edgar` is robots-disallowed; company IR filing stores are the "has this been filed" route (two independent store reads = strongest available evidence, still not proof). In the cloud sandbox `www.sec.gov/Archives/` is reachable by the fetch tool but not by `curl`.
- **Robots/host limits found:** `brand.adyen.com` (Adyen statements PDFs), CAREL's IR file server (unparseable binary), `release.tdnet.info`, `investors.hexagon.com`, `s204.q4cdn.com`, `assets.asics.com`, `api.alertir.com`, `mb.cision.com`. Clean: Intuit, PSI, InPost, Symbotic IR stores; kabupro for EDINET (standing).
- **Standing rules worth keeping:** one issuer per corporate-action query (grouped queries return nothing); carry the **new** date, never the old one with "(extended)"; before recording a single-figure finding that contradicts the corpus, re-fetch the row as printed with headers and units (a 10× unit misread on NKE's cash-flow line was caught this way); never infer a historical balance-sheet movement from a forward-looking guidance line (CAREL/Cotes); a supersession that drops a section without saying so is not a supersession.

### E. Scheduled-run architecture — findings that supersede the "Overnight / delayed-run design" sketch below (detail: `queue/runs/2026-09-09_scheduled-runs-postmortem-and-workflow-fix_v1.md`)

- **The block is per-session repo attachment, not GitHub.** In the cloud sandbox `api.github.com` returns 403 "GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session. Use `add_repo`"; `add_repo` does not exist in the tool registry; `git push --dry-run` returns 403 "not in this session's authorized repository set" — **the PAT is stripped and irrelevant to the write path.** `git clone` / `raw.githubusercontent.com` reads work with the PAT; a command classifier blocks some `bash` shapes non-deterministically. None of the six scheduled tasks had a repository source; all six were disabled 2026-09-09 13:37 UTC.
- **Fix (user actions):** recreate the tasks with the repo attached as a GitHub source (or device-bound); collapse six runs to one daily (~07:30–09:00 UTC, after Asia settles) + one weekly integrity run; prompts point at `queue/TODO.md`, name stop conditions, and never write working state to project knowledge; **hard stop: no live `MODEL.md` read → no verdict, no memo, no register version** (bootstrap v1.1.0 change — manual paste, framework gate); mirror MODEL v4.2.0 into project knowledge for mobile reading; add `queue/TODO.md` and `queue/IDEAS.md` to BOOTSTRAP §CONFIG; **rotate/revoke the PAT** (now in ~27 transcripts; open since 2026-08-27).

2. ~~**TRI (Thomson Reuters) — NCIB expiry, 18 Aug 2026**~~ → **v3 committed. The bid expired unrenewed.** No successor authorisation, no substitute mechanism, verified by two independent retrievals of the IR index. CAUTION confidence 70% → **87%** after a 28 Aug EDGAR sweep of every filing by the registrant in August returned no NCIB document. WATCH held. **New adverse alert: lease liabilities of $241m were retrieved from the Q2 6-K's XBRL notes, pulling the conviction line $116.04 → $115.49 and the band $27.23/$28.55 → $26.68/$28.00 — so the price now sits 9.3% below the line, not 10.8%.** The P17 trailing-3y NI leg is also computed rather than bounded (FY2023–25 sourced).
3. ~~**STMN.SW (Straumann) — H1-2026, 19 Aug 2026**~~ → **v3: VERDICT FLIPPED, WATCH → AVOID, SF PASS → FAIL.** The first pass concluded Straumann publishes no interim financial statements and held WATCH. **That was wrong.** The ad hoc results release is itself a ~30-page document carrying full IAS-34 condensed statements (balance sheet p.15, income statement p.16, cash flow pp.18–19, equity statement p.20, notes 1–12), at a hash-ID URL not guessable from the annual-report path and invisible on the EQS mirror that search surfaces first. They show **treasury purchases of CHF 69,328k in H1 2026** — 2.8× the CHF 24,559k spent in all of FY2025, and 4.2× the CHF 16,605k handed back to plans in the same half — taking the treasury balance from CHF 0.4m to CHF 53.2m, **with no purpose stated anywhere in the document** (two independent full-document searches). Both premises of the CAUTION are falsified: it is not plan-coverage scale, and the treasury account is not a 4,434-share pass-through. All three ADVERSE conjuncts now hold, and §2·1C makes an ADVERSE capital-allocation verdict an SF FAIL in itself. **Confidence ~72%**; the single dissent line is whether unexplained accumulation with no announced programme satisfies "the programme executing." Also new: **CEO transition announced 19 Aug — Christopher Norbye effective 1 Dec 2026**, Daniellot out end-Nov after seven years. Three §4 extraction conflicts resolved. It also resolved the CEO-comp extraction conflict with a cause ($36,845,552 SCT Total vs $36,835,552 CD&A recap excluding $10,000 other comp) and surfaced **a new severity-changing INDETERMINATE: founder-director Scott Cook at $1,300,000, all "other compensation", 1.9× the ordinary-seat stop where 2× is a hard strike.** The November proxy now carries two ways to fail SF.

6. ~~**HEXA-B.ST — Q2 2026 re-base, 29 Jul 2026**~~ → **v3 committed. SF FAIL → PASS; AVOID narrows from two gates to one (Past WEAK alone).** v2's P19 strike was a **pre-v4 mechanical sterilisation ladder, not a §2·1C·3 judgment** — re-run on the current test, conjunct (iii) fails outright: **no repurchases since 2023**, treasury frozen at **21,100,000 shares** across both reporting dates, and no purchase line in the H1 2026 cash flow. **Past cannot be re-based, and will not be until FY2027.** Hexagon restated the income statement and cash flow to continuing operations but **not the balance sheet** — the 31 Dec 2025 column is whole-group — so no post-Octave ROCE exists for any completed year and the first comparable pair is FY2027. Both v18 traps held: H1 operating earnings of 2,072.4 MEUR include a **+1,588.0 MEUR capital gain** (Q2-only 251.4 is the clean read), and the word "restated" appears nowhere in the report. Band **SEK 8.95 / 17.84** (99% wide), Dist **+470%**, price SEK 101.75. **EUR/SEK 11.1295 is an external input** — the company states no rate, so every EUR→SEK conversion in the memo is labelled input, not sourced.

7. ~~**GAP — Q2 FY2026, ~26 Aug 2026**~~ → **v2 committed. AVOID on two gates held.** No turnaround: the $676m operating income is **≈$259m ex the $417m IEEPA tariff credit — down ~11%** — on sales −2% and comps −1%; adjusted gross margin +20bp. The brand split corroborates Future WEAK rather than challenging it: Old Navy (>50% of sales) −4% and Athleta −12%, against Gap +10% and Banana Republic +3%. **P19 moves to CAUTION, a flag v1 did not carry**: $601m of buybacks in 26 weeks is **3.9× all of FY2025**, paid alongside $125m of dividends against **$261m of H1 free cash flow** (2.8×), at ~$23.48 with no published criterion — one flag, unpaired, so SF still PASS. **v1's chair-fee caveat is withdrawn** on superseded calibration (chair stop ≈$1.02m against Shattuck's $525,992; the 2026 proxy director table was retrieved in the primary). Band re-anchored on the 1 Aug balance sheet to **$5.76 / $12.62**; **Dist +65% — the narrowest in the batch**, and said so in the memo because price could plausibly arrive before quality does. **Open:** the Q2 10-Q is still unfiled — the Item 5 monthly buyback table converts the derived ~$23.48 into a disclosed average, and is days away.

**STILL OPEN:**

8. ~~**EA outside date 28 Sep 2026**~~ → **CLOSED at register v280.** Take-private by PIF / Silver Lake / Affinity Partners **completed 4 Aug 2026 at $210.00 cash/sh**; common stock ceased trading, delisted from Nasdaq [sourced: EA release 4 Aug 2026, `ea.com/news/ea-announces-completion-of-acquisition`; corroborated `pif.gov.sa`]. Row moved AVOID → DNAF terminal; not held, so no tax event.

9. **PYPL — full REVIEW owed: lapse branch fired 27–28 Aug 2026.** Stripe/Advent abandoned the pursuit [press-sourced: Bloomberg 27 Aug, Axios 28 Aug, Reuters 28 Aug; no 8-K or company statement located — same standing as the approach itself]. De-pinned price: **$53.66 close 28 Aug 2026 (−12.7%)** [sourced: Yahoo Finance]; $56.82 close 3 Sep [2026-09-04 run, stockanalysis]. **v14's "pre-bid low-$40s inside the all-cash band" premise is struck** — the price did not return there; whether ≈$54–57 sits inside CHEAP ≈$33.96–47.12 is a framework question owed at the REVIEW, not an inherited conclusion.

## Re-seeding the ANALYSE queue — user decision (option (c) now carries stock, v17)

- **(a) Run the named re-test backlog above as REVIEW work** — highest value per hour; every name is a delta, not a build. **Still the recommended first call**: the ~50-name backlog is dated, verdict-moving and already half-sourced, where every name in the two new blocks is a cold build.
- **(b) The corporate-actions sweep** — cheap, mechanical, prevents quiet rot. **Now overdue and demonstrably load-bearing:** the 2026-08-28 pass over the candidate block alone caught three live take-privates (Rotork, Intertek, Steadfast) and a fourth already inside the register's own ABB row. Two of the last fifteen backlog names had already ceased to exist as listings. The same rot is running through the 319 live rows unchecked.
- **(c) A new ANALYSE market block** — **no longer an open question: 239 names are now queued above**, 58 from the top-100 coverage audit and 181 from the re-seed universe, across fifteen markets. Utilities are not seeded (standing screen). Sequencing is a user call; the note under Flags sets out what the register's shape argues for.

## Coverage audit — global top 100 by market cap (snapshot 2026-08-28)

*Source: companiesmarketcap.com global ranking, retrieved 2026-08-28 (sourced data; ranks only — **no market-cap figures are carried into this file**, and the ranking itself is a third-party aggregation, tier-3 under §4, adequate for a coverage checklist and for nothing else). Matched against all 317 register rows on **company name, not the ticker key** (§7.1a) — cross-listings resolved, so Roche→`ROG.SW`, Novartis→`NVS`, Tencent→`0700.HK`, TSMC→`2330.TW`, Novo Nordisk→`NOVO-B.CO` all count as covered.*

**Result: 42 of the top 100 carry a register row; 58 do not.** The gap is not random — it is concentrated in exactly the three groups the project has never seeded: mega-cap banks and insurers, the semiconductor complex, and state-controlled Asian and Gulf issuers.

**Framing, stated plainly: this is a coverage audit, not a candidate screen.** Size is neither a quality nor a cheapness signal, and nothing here has been pre-judged on any gate. The value of working the list is that each absence becomes a recorded decision instead of an oversight — an AVOID row saves future time under §7.1 exactly as a WATCH row does.

### Tier B — financials, FI lens (MODEL v4.5.0 J4) — 12 names

**Each runs the Gate 0 lens test first (MODEL v4.5.0, J4).** A licensed bank or insurer, or the parent of a group supervised as one, that publishes a capital ratio covering ≥ 90% of consolidated assets takes the FI lens: ROE and capital tests (P24), MCap/NI ≤ 8, the P25 band. A bank or insurer with a material non-financial part, or without that coverage, is too hard (R4: Gate 0 FAIL, DNAF). The §2·1A substitution rule of the KSPI v1→v2 lesson (TODO v13) is retired. Every name here is a deposit- or premium-funded balance sheet. **Superseded in part by MODEL v4.6.0 (G2):** the too-hard pile is retired; a bank or insurer that does not fit FI is read on STD with its conservative rules, never DNAF.

## ANALYSE queue re-seed — candidate universe (built 2026-08-28)

*Answers the standing re-seed question below, option (c). **181 names, none of which has a register row** — de-duplicated against all 317 rows live at the 2026-08-28 snapshot, on company name per §7.1a, not on the ticker key. **Re-verified 2026-08-31 against the two rows added since** — SEE.L (Seeing Machines) and FLUT (Flutter Entertainment): neither appears in either block, and a core-name sweep of all 319 live company names against both blocks returns no collision. Tickers are suggested keys, resolved and verified with ISIN at run time. Selection rule applied: understandable business · long continuous record under a stable perimeter · structurally decent returns or a durable niche · family/founder/anchor control welcome. Screened out at source: utilities and regulated networks (standing screen), pure-play miners, oil & gas E&P, REITs, closed-end funds and pure investment holdings, SPAC-derived names, and anything with under ~5 years of standalone statements. **No figures are carried here by design** — nothing below is sourced financial data, and no name below has been valued, screened for cheapness, or pre-judged on any gate.*

## Overnight / delayed-run design (draft — for later recycling)

Design sketch for an unattended runner that processes this queue on a schedule. Not built yet; finalise later.

**Substrate.** Claude Code in headless mode (`claude -p`) driven by a **scheduled GitHub Action** (`on: schedule: cron`). This reuses the existing repo-native pipeline — `MODEL.md` as the operative framework, `_REGISTER.md` as state, §7.4a as the commit protocol — rather than re-plumbing it into a bespoke script. Same agent loop (bash, web fetch, git), no human in the loop. Alternatives: plain `cron` on a local/cloud box running the same `claude -p` call, or Claude Code Routines for clock-triggered runs. Cost for a nightly list of a few dozen names is single-digit dollars.

**Flow.**
1. Runner reads this file's queue section (mode + name).
2. Session-start: load PAT from secret, fetch live `MODEL.md` + `_REGISTER.md`.
3. Process each name **strictly sequentially** through the five-gate pipeline.
4. Commit each memo, then merge the register — one runner, sequential writes only.
5. Clear processed names from the queue; emit a JSON run summary (`--output-format json`, per-name verdict + `total_cost_usd`).

**Non-negotiable guardrails for unattended operation.**
- **Data-completeness gate.** If the inputs for the four CE variants / cheapness legs are not cleanly sourced, emit an `INCOMPLETE` stub memo flagged for human review and skip the verdict. Never fill a gap with an assumption to finish a row.
- **Sequential register I/O.** No fan-out. Parallel jobs PUT-ing `_REGISTER.md` manufacture exactly the collisions §7.4a exists to catch.
- **MODEL-mode lockout.** Runner handles ANALYSE / REVIEW / SWEEP only. Framework changes stay a human-only, exact-phrase "commit model" gate; runner has no write access to `model/` (was `instructions/` before MODEL v4.2.1).
- **Commit-time compliance re-measure** (v4.2.0): P13 word count, §7.1 cell caps, four-gates-only check, no register-wide superlatives — unattended runs drift ~10–65% over P13 and their own in-run compliance claims are not evidence.
- Secrets (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, PAT) as repo secrets, never in workflow files; `--allowedTools` scoped to EDGAR / Yahoo / GitHub.
- **Retrieval doctrine from the Aug-2026 runs:** primary results releases are the backbone (a Q4 release carries two full years of statements in one document and survives the URL-must-be-surfaced-by-search constraint); tier-2 columns must tie to a primary figure before their headers are trusted; per-filer productive channels are logged in each memo's App. A (e.g. Hitachi results library; Panasonic press-release PDFs; MHI tanshin archive; Lagardère interim reports; Trigano results releases). **US-domestic-filer addendum (FLUT, 2026-08-31):** for SEC filers the **10-Q is the efficient primary route**, not the 10-K — Item 1 Financial Statements is the first content in a 10-Q and therefore inside the fetch window, while a 10-K's Item 8 sits ~80 pages past it and truncates away. A 10-Q's comparative columns carry the prior fiscal year-end balance sheet in full, which alone satisfies three of the four §4 tie-out anchors plus the net-financial-position composite. Also logged: in this sandbox `bash` cannot reach `data.sec.gov` (proxy `host_not_allowed`) and `web_fetch` rejects any URL not first surfaced by a search, so the XBRL companyfacts/companyconcept endpoints are unavailable — plan the retrieval around searchable filing URLs.

**The real bottleneck is data, not scheduling.** The SEC-filer path is deterministic and safe to automate. The European and Japanese non-SEC names depend on primary-report PDF extraction — the fragile, judgment-heavy path where an unattended run is most likely to silently produce a wrong verdict. Harden those fetchers (or route them to `INCOMPLETE`) before trusting overnight output on this universe.

## Flags


- **Korean primary filings are unreachable from the sandbox (logged 2026-09-04, 244920.KS v1):** `dart.fss.or.kr` and `wcomp/comp.fnguide.com` refuse on robots, `kind.krx.co.kr` on bot detection, the issuer's own IR site on robots; `englishdart` was not reachable either. Every `.KS` ANALYSE in the re-seed block (9 names) and any KSPI-style Korean-listed name will hit the same wall — tier-2 series can be built (stockanalysis/S&P carries 5y for KRX) but cannot be accepted under §4. **Options for the user:** (a) an OpenDART API key (`opendart.fss.or.kr`, free registration) — would need `opendart.fss.or.kr` added to the sandbox allow-list; (b) Korean primaries uploaded to the chat as PDF/HTML for the session; (c) accept provisional tier-2 memos for Korean names with a standing rebuild flag, as done for 244920.KS. Until decided, Korean ANALYSE output carries a provisional label.
- **⚠ USER DECISION — rotate the GitHub PAT.** Three independent retrieval agents, working on unrelated names and none prompted to look, each surfaced the same observation this session: **the fine-grained PAT is stored in plaintext in the Claude project instructions**, where it is visible to anything that reads that context. The bootstrap's own terms already require rotation "after sharing any chat or this project, and on any suspicion of exposure." Nothing indicates misuse, and the token's blast radius is bounded by design (single repo, Contents read/write only, ≤90-day expiry) — but three unprompted flags is itself the suspicion the term contemplates. **Recommended: rotate at github.com/settings/personal-access-tokens, update the token line in the project instructions, and re-paste.** No repo change is needed and no session action is taken without the user's instruction. **✓ CLOSED 2026-09-12 — the PAT was rotated after the Cowork probe run exposed the v1.1.1 token line in a hosted transcript; BOOTSTRAP v1.2.0 carries the new line and records the rotation date. The flag text below is kept as the record of why.** *(Raised 2026-08-27. **Was still open 2026-09-09** — the token is now in ~27 transcripts and was used successfully from a cloud sandbox by three scheduled runs; the post-mortem in `queue/runs/` recommends revoking it outright once the scheduled tasks are recreated with the repo attached.)*
- **Retrieval-environment constraint — new, and it shapes what can be scheduled.** Three classes of primary source refused this session: **network-policy 403s** at the egress proxy (assets.asics.com, api.alertir.com, mb.cision.com, data.sec.gov, efts.sec.gov), **robots.txt blocks** (release.tdnet.info, sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar), and **fetch-approval gates** that time out unanswered in an unattended run (investors.hexagon.com, s204.q4cdn.com, sec.gov/Archives directory listings, several live-quote pages). Company IR sites that render via JavaScript (Straumann, Zip) return empty shells. **Consequence for the overnight-runner design below: the data-completeness gate is not a theoretical guardrail — it fired twice today**, on Hexagon and Gap, and would have produced two wrong verdicts had it not. Log which hosts work per filer in each memo's Appendix A, as the retrieval doctrine already requires.
- **✓ "Cartara" → Certara (CERT) — CONFIRMED by user 2026-08-18.** Flag closed; the CERT row stands (AVOID, two gates, analysed 2026-08-09).
- **Bed Bath & Beyond** — resolved by evidence, analysed as **BBBY** (AVOID, three gates); **NXH re-key pending** the rename's effectiveness (see Corporate actions).

Germany has 34 rows and the UK 6, against comparable universes of quality industrials — that gap is an artefact of the block order the project happened to run, not a judgment anyone made. The UK, Canada and Australia blocks are where the marginal name is most likely to be both new and analysable on existing machinery.

- **v20 — 2026-09-01** — KSPI v3 committed at register v279. **A user-directed lens change, not a business change: WATCH → AVOID.** The §2·1A financial-institution substitution was set aside and the industrial machinery run instead; Past fails on leverage (L/E 340% FY2025 / 353% 1H26 against the 200% flag, with P6's carve-out unmet because the borrowing is retail deposits funding a loan book, not repurchase funding) and independently on a three-year uninterrupted ROCE decline. **The lens is the whole flip** — no deposit-taker passes the literal leverage test, and the balance-sheet condition v2 relied on still holds. Routed to IDEAS A2, of which this is now the most consequential instance: the first where the two readings give opposite *final verdicts* rather than one gate. **Three method findings.** (1) The user's directed cash-flow check was right to run and did not bear out as suspected — v2's FY2025 FCF of ₸491,098m correctly excluded the Hepsiburada consideration — but it exposed a larger problem: reported CFO embeds the loan-book/deposit gap, so FCF swings ₸1,056bn → ₸486bn → ₸491bn → ₸953bn on trading that did not move that way. TTM FCF is **₸952,971m**, taking MCap/FCF from v2's 18.7× to **9.6×**. Both bases now carried and labelled at every use. (2) **v2's EV/OI "n.m., 5.4×–11.4× spread" was an inconsistency, not uncertainty** — deposits added to EV while their interest cost stayed deducted from the denominator. Paired financing-blind the metric is 6.7×–7.8× and becomes the binding band leg. Worth sweeping any other memo that marked EV/OI n.m. on a deposit funder. (3) **A v2 data gap was a retrieval miss, not a disclosure gap:** Kaspi furnishes two 6-K exhibits within days of each other, and the interim financial-information exhibit carries a full IAS-34 cash-flow statement, the NCI split and the buy-back share count that v2 recorded as unavailable. Standing lesson: for foreign private issuers, check for a second 6-K before recording a disclosure gap. Band ≈$98 → ≈₸39,000/$86; Dist +8% → +21%. All figures computed in tenge — third-party USD/KZT quotes for late August diverge 455–477, so USD uses the ADS-implied cross of 454.89 and distances are FX-invariant. **Re-merged onto live head:** the session base was v18; a parallel 2026-08-28 recovery landed v19 (the 239-name coverage-audit and re-seed blocks) between fetch and PUT, and §7.4a base verification caught it — renumbered v19 → v20 with no work lost on either side. v19 archived → `queue/archive/TODO_v19_2026-08-31.md`.
